By <a href="/wiki/William_Henry_Bartlett" title="William Henry Bartlett">W. H. Bartlett</a></div></div></div>
<p>93% of Palestinians are Muslim,<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> the vast majority of whom are followers of the <a href="/wiki/Sunni" class="mw-redirect" title="Sunni">Sunni</a> branch of <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Islam</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> with a small minority of <a href="/wiki/Ahmadiyya" title="Ahmadiyya">Ahmadiyya</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> and 15% being <a href="/wiki/Nondenominational_Muslims" class="mw-redirect" title="Nondenominational Muslims">nondenominational Muslims</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Palestinian_Christian" class="mw-redirect" title="Palestinian Christian">Palestinian Christians</a> represent a significant minority of 6%, followed by much smaller <a href="/wiki/Religion" title="Religion">religious</a> communities, including <a href="/wiki/Druze" title="Druze">Druze</a><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2017)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> and <a href="/wiki/Samaritans" title="Samaritans">Samaritans</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Salome2010_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Salome2010-135">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_the_State_of_Palestine" title="Economy of the State of Palestine">Economy of the State of Palestine</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Taxation_in_the_State_of_Palestine" title="Taxation in the State of Palestine">Taxation in the State of Palestine</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_the_State_of_Palestine" title="Tourism in the State of Palestine">Tourism in the State of Palestine</a></div>
<p>Tourism in the territory claimed by the State of Palestine refers to <a href="/wiki/Tourism" title="Tourism">tourism</a> in <a href="/wiki/East_Jerusalem" title="East Jerusalem">East Jerusalem</a>, the <a href="/wiki/West_Bank" title="West Bank">West Bank</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Gaza_Strip" title="Gaza Strip">Gaza Strip</a>. In 2010, 4.6 million people visited the Palestinian territories, compared to 2.6 million in 2009. Of that number, 2.2 million were foreign tourists while 2.7 million were domestic.<sup id="cite_ref-PCBS_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PCBS-136">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Most tourists come for only a few hours or as part of a day trip itinerary. In the last quarter of 2012 over 150,000 guests stayed in West Bank hotels; 40% were European and 9% were from the United States and Canada.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> Lonely Planet travel guide writes that "the West Bank is not the easiest place in which to travel but the effort is richly rewarded."<sup id="cite_ref-:0_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-138">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> In 2013 Palestinian Authority Tourism minister <a href="/w/index.php?title=Rula_Ma%27ay%27a&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Rula Ma&#39;ay&#39;a (page does not exist)">Rula Ma'ay'a</a> stated that her government aims to encourage international visits to Palestine, but the occupation is the main factor preventing the tourism sector from becoming a major income source to Palestinians.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> There are no visa conditions imposed on foreign nationals other than those imposed by&#160;the visa policy of Israel. Access to&#160;Jerusalem, the&#160;West Bank, and Gaza&#160;is completely controlled by the Government of Israel. Entry to the occupied Palestinian territories requires only a valid international passport.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Communications">Communications</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Communications_in_the_State_of_Palestine" title="Communications in the State of Palestine">Communications in the State of Palestine</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Palestinian_Central_Bureau_of_Statistics" title="Palestinian Central Bureau of Statistics">Palestinian Central Bureau of Statistics</a> (PCBS) and the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Telecom_and_Information_Technology_of_the_State_of_Palestine" title="Ministry of Telecom and Information Technology of the State of Palestine">Ministry of Telecom and Information Technology</a> said there were 4.2 million cellular mobile subscribers in Palestine compared to 2.6 million at the end of 2010 while the number of ADSL subscribers in Palestine increased to about 363 thousand by the end of 2019 from 119 thousand over the same period. 97% of Palestinian households have at least one cellular mobile line while at least one smartphone is owned by 86% of households (91% in the West Bank and 78% in Gaza Strip). About 80% of the Palestinian households have access to the internet in their homes and about a third have a computer.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
On 12 June 2020, the World Bank approved a US$15 million grant for the Technology for Youth and Jobs (TechStart) Project aiming to help the Palestinian IT sector upgrade the capabilities of firms and create more high-quality jobs. Kanthan Shankar, World Bank Country Director for West Bank and Gaza said "The IT sector has the potential to make a strong contribution to economic growth. It can offer opportunities to Palestinian youth, who constitute 30% of the population and suffer from acute unemployment."<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Financial_services">Financial services</span></h3>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Palestine_Monetary_Authority" title="Palestine Monetary Authority">Palestine Monetary Authority</a> has issued guidelines for the operation and provision of electronic payment services including e-wallet and prepaid cards.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transportation">Transportation</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_the_State_of_Palestine" title="Transport in the State of Palestine">Transport in the State of Palestine</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Water_supply_and_sanitation">Water supply and sanitation</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Water_supply_and_sanitation_in_the_State_of_Palestine" title="Water supply and sanitation in the State of Palestine">Water supply and sanitation in the State of Palestine</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Water,_Sanitation_and_Hygiene_Monitoring_Program" title="Water, Sanitation and Hygiene Monitoring Program">Water, Sanitation and Hygiene Monitoring Program</a></div>
<p>Water supply and sanitation in the Palestinian territories are characterized by severe water shortage and are highly influenced by the Israeli occupation. The water resources of Palestine are fully controlled by Israel and the division of groundwater is subject to provisions in the <a href="/wiki/Oslo_II_Accord" title="Oslo II Accord">Oslo II Accord</a>.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Generally, the water quality is considerably worse in the <a href="/wiki/Gaza_strip" class="mw-redirect" title="Gaza strip">Gaza strip</a> when compared to the <a href="/wiki/West_Bank" title="West Bank">West Bank</a>. About a third to half of the delivered water in the Palestinian territories is <a href="/wiki/Non-revenue_water" title="Non-revenue water">lost in the distribution network</a>. The lasting blockade of the Gaza Strip and the <a href="/wiki/Gaza_War_(2008%E2%80%9309)" class="mw-redirect" title="Gaza War (2008–09)">Gaza War</a> have caused severe damage to the infrastructure in the Gaza Strip.<sup id="cite_ref-UN_Gaza_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-UN_Gaza-144">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
Concerning wastewater, the existing treatment plants do not have the capacity to treat all of the produced wastewater, causing severe water pollution.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The development of the sector highly depends on external financing.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Palestine" title="Culture of Palestine">Culture of Palestine</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_the_State_of_Palestine" title="List of World Heritage Sites in the State of Palestine">List of World Heritage Sites in the State of Palestine</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Media">Media</span></h3>
<p>There are a number of newspapers, news agencies, and satellite television stations in the State of Palestine. Its news agencies include <a href="/wiki/Ma%27an_News_Agency" title="Ma&#39;an News Agency">Ma'an News Agency</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wafa" title="Wafa">Wafa</a>, Palestine News Network. <a href="/wiki/Al-Aqsa_TV" title="Al-Aqsa TV">Al-Aqsa TV</a>, <a href="/wiki/Al-Quds_TV" title="Al-Quds TV">Al-Quds TV</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sanabel_TV" title="Sanabel TV">Sanabel TV</a> are its main satellite broadcasters.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sports_in_Palestine" class="mw-redirect" title="Sports in Palestine">Sports in Palestine</a></div>
<p><a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">Association football</a> (soccer) is the most popular sport among the Palestinian people.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> The <a href="/wiki/Palestine_national_football_team" title="Palestine national football team">Palestine national football team</a> represents the state in international football. <a href="/wiki/Rugby_football" title="Rugby football">Rugby</a> is also a popular sport.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (May 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Art.2C_music.2C_and_clothing"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Art,_music,_and_clothing">Art, music, and clothing</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Palestinian_art" title="Palestinian art">Palestinian art</a>, <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Palestine" title="Music of Palestine">Music of Palestine</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Palestinian_traditional_costumes" title="Palestinian traditional costumes">Palestinian traditional costumes</a></div>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Notes">Notes</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1011085734"/><div class="reflist reflist-columns references-column-width reflist-lower-alpha" style="column-width: 45em;">
<ol class="references">
<li id="cite_note-naming-1"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-naming_1-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-naming_1-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">Note that the name <a href="/wiki/Palestine_(region)" title="Palestine (region)">Palestine</a> can commonly be interpreted as the entire territory of the former <a href="/wiki/Mandatory_Palestine" title="Mandatory Palestine">British Mandate</a>, which today also incorporates Israel. The name is also officially used as the short-form reference to the State of Palestine<sup id="cite_ref-Bissiop433_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bissiop433-19">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> and this should be distinguished from other homonymous uses for the term including the Palestinian Authority,<sup id="cite_ref-Pagep161_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Pagep161-20">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> the Palestine Liberation Organization,<sup id="cite_ref-GA43177_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GA43177-21">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> and the subject of other <a href="/wiki/Proposals_for_a_Palestinian_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Proposals for a Palestinian state">proposals for the establishment of a Palestinian state</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-4"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-4">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">The <a href="/wiki/Palestinian_Declaration_of_Independence" title="Palestinian Declaration of Independence">Palestinian Declaration of Independence</a> proclaims the "establishment of the State of Palestine on our Palestinian territory with its capital Jerusalem (Al-Quds Ash-Sharif)." Israel exercises <i>de facto</i> control over Jerusalem, but Palestine and Israel's claims to Jerusalem are <a href="/wiki/Status_of_Jerusalem" title="Status of Jerusalem">not recognized by the international community</a>. <a href="/wiki/Ramallah" title="Ramallah">Ramallah</a> is the administrative capital where government institutions and <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_to_Palestine" class="mw-redirect" title="List of diplomatic missions to Palestine">foreign representative offices</a> are located, while most countries maintain their <a href="/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_in_Israel" title="List of diplomatic missions in Israel">embassies to Israel</a> in <a href="/wiki/Tel_Aviv" title="Tel Aviv">Tel Aviv</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-control-6"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-control_6-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-control_6-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-control_6-2"><sup><i><b>c</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-control_6-3"><sup><i><b>d</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">Israel allows the PNA to execute some functions in the Palestinian territories, depending on the <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_the_Oslo_Accords" class="mw-redirect" title="Administrative divisions of the Oslo Accords">area classification</a>. It maintains minimal interference (retaining control of borders: <a href="/wiki/National_airspace" class="mw-redirect" title="National airspace">air</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-GazaSeaAir_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GazaSeaAir-23">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> sea beyond <a href="/wiki/Internal_waters" title="Internal waters">internal waters</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-GazaSeaAir_23-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GazaSeaAir-23">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/European_Union_Border_Assistance_Mission_Rafah" class="mw-redirect" title="European Union Border Assistance Mission Rafah">land</a><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>) in the Gaza Strip (its interior and Egypt portion of the land border are under <a href="/wiki/Governance_of_the_Gaza_Strip" title="Governance of the Gaza Strip">Hamas control</a>), and varying degrees of interference elsewhere.<sup id="cite_ref-HumanRightsWatch_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HumanRightsWatch-26">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> See also <a href="/wiki/Israeli-occupied_territories" title="Israeli-occupied territories">Israeli-occupied territories</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-PLOchair-17"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-PLOchair_17-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-PLOchair_17-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text">So far both presidents of the State of Palestine, <a href="/wiki/Yasser_Arafat" title="Yasser Arafat">Yasser Arafat</a> and his successor <a href="/wiki/Mahmoud_Abbas" title="Mahmoud Abbas">Mahmoud Abbas</a>, were appointed beforehand as <a href="/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Executive_Committee_of_the_Palestine_Liberation_Organization" class="mw-redirect" title="Chairman of the Executive Committee of the Palestine Liberation Organization">Chairman of the Executive Committee of the Palestine Liberation Organization</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Executive_Committee_of_the_Palestine_Liberation_Organization" title="Executive Committee of the Palestine Liberation Organization">committee</a> performing the functions of State of Palestine government.<sup id="cite_ref-A67L28_69-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-A67L28-69">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-GiE_76-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-GiE-76">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> See also <a href="/wiki/Leaders_of_Palestinian_institutions" title="Leaders of Palestinian institutions">Leaders of Palestinian institutions</a>.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-42"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-42">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In his September 2011 speech to the United Nations arguing for full membership, Mahmoud Abbas said "... we agreed to establish the State of Palestine on only 22% of the territory of historical Palestine—on all the Palestinian Territory occupied by Israel in 1967."<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div>

<li id="cite_note-16"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-16">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">According to <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="http://www.mfa.gov.il/MFA/Peace+Process/Guide+to+the+Peace+Process/Gaza-Jericho+Agreement+Annex+IV+-+Economic+Protoco.htm">Article 4 of the 1994 Paris Protocol</a> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20161001065601/http://www.mfa.gov.il/MFA/Peace%2BProcess/Guide%2Bto%2Bthe%2BPeace%2BProcess/Gaza-Jericho%2BAgreement%2BAnnex%2BIV%2B-%2BEconomic%2BProtoco.htm">Archived</a> 1 October 2016 at the <a href="/wiki/Wayback_Machine" title="Wayback Machine">Wayback Machine</a>. The Protocol allows the Palestinian Authority to adopt multiple currencies. In the <a href="/wiki/West_Bank" title="West Bank">West Bank</a>, the Israeli new sheqel and Jordanian dinar are widely accepted; while in the <a href="/wiki/Gaza_Strip" title="Gaza Strip">Gaza Strip</a>, the Israeli new sheqel and Egyptian pound are widely accepted.</span>

<li id="cite_note-GiE-76"><span class="mw-cite-backlink">^ <a href="#cite_ref-GiE_76-0"><sup><i><b>a</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-GiE_76-1"><sup><i><b>b</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-GiE_76-2"><sup><i><b>c</b></i></sup></a> <a href="#cite_ref-GiE_76-3"><sup><i><b>d</b></i></sup></a></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFSayigh1999" class="citation book cs1">Sayigh, Yezid (1999). <i>Armed Struggle and the Search for State: The Palestinian National Movement, 1949–1993</i> (illustrated&#160;ed.). <a href="/wiki/Oxford_University_Press" title="Oxford University Press">Oxford University Press</a>. p.&#160;624. <a href="/wiki/ISBN_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="ISBN (identifier)">ISBN</a>&#160;<a href="/wiki/Special:BookSources/978-0-19-829643-0" title="Special:BookSources/978-0-19-829643-0"><bdi>978-0-19-829643-0</bdi></a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=book&amp;rft.btitle=Armed+Struggle+and+the+Search+for+State%3A+The+Palestinian+National+Movement%2C+1949%E2%80%931993&amp;rft.pages=624&amp;rft.edition=illustrated&amp;rft.pub=Oxford+University+Press&amp;rft.date=1999&amp;rft.isbn=978-0-19-829643-0&amp;rft.aulast=Sayigh&amp;rft.aufirst=Yezid&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AState+of+Palestine" class="Z3988"></span> "The Palestinian National Council also empowered the central council to form a government-in-exile when appropriate, and the <a href="/wiki/Executive_Committee_of_the_Palestine_Liberation_Organization" title="Executive Committee of the Palestine Liberation Organization">executive committee</a> to perform the functions of government until such time as a government-in-exile was established."</span>

<li id="cite_note-112"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-112">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite class="citation web cs1"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.nytimes.com/2015/01/06/world/middleeast/q-and-a-on-palestinian-statehood.html">"Q. and A. on Palestinian Statehood"</a>. NYT. 5 January 2015<span class="reference-accessdate">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap">10 October</span> 2022</span>. <q>Q. Does Palestine meet that legal definition [Montevideo Convention]? A.Many experts say that it does, though there is considerable complexity surrounding the particular criteria. &amp; Q. What about recognition? A.The Palestinian leadership has been trying for years to garner as much international recognition as possible, hoping to achieve a diplomatic critical mass that would force the hand of Israel and the United States. By November 2012, when the United Nations General Assembly voted to upgrade Palestine's status to that of a nonmember observer state, 132 of the 198 members of the United Nations recognized Palestinian statehood. Several more have done so since then.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=Q.+and+A.+on+Palestinian+Statehood&amp;rft.pub=NYT&amp;rft.date=2015-01-05&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.nytimes.com%2F2015%2F01%2F06%2Fworld%2Fmiddleeast%2Fq-and-a-on-palestinian-statehood.html&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AState+of+Palestine" class="Z3988"></span></span>

<li id="cite_note-114"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-114">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1133582631"/><cite id="CITEREFVictor_Matthew_Kattan" class="citation book cs1">Victor Matthew Kattan. "The Statehood of Palestine: A Constitutivist View (May 21, 2021)".  In Hani Sayed &amp;Thomas Skouteris (ed.). <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://dx.doi.org/10.2139/ssrn.3850777"><i>Oxford Handbook on International Law in the Arab World</i></a>. Oxford University Press, Forthcoming. <a href="/wiki/Doi_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="Doi (identifier)">doi</a>:<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://doi.org/10.2139%2Fssrn.3850777">10.2139/ssrn.3850777</a>. <a href="/wiki/S2CID_(identifier)" class="mw-redirect" title="S2CID (identifier)">S2CID</a>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://api.semanticscholar.org/CorpusID:241140129">241140129</a>. <q>It would be difficult to deny that the recognition of Palestine by over two-thirds of the member states of the United Nations (72 per cent of members), its membership of seventeen international organizations, three international courts and its accession to many major multilateral treaties, in pursuit of Palestinian self-determination, means that Palestine is a state under international law even though its territory continues to be occupied – illegally – by the Government of Israel</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=bookitem&amp;rft.atitle=The+Statehood+of+Palestine%3A+A+Constitutivist+View+%28May+21%2C+2021%29&amp;rft.btitle=Oxford+Handbook+on+International+Law+in+the+Arab+World&amp;rft.pub=Oxford+University+Press%2C+Forthcoming&amp;rft_id=info%3Adoi%2F10.2139%2Fssrn.3850777&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fapi.semanticscholar.org%2FCorpusID%3A241140129%23id-name%3DS2CID&amp;rft.au=Victor+Matthew+Kattan&amp;rft_id=http%3A%2F%2Fdx.doi.org%2F10.2139%2Fssrn.3850777&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AState+of+Palestine" class="Z3988"></span></span>

"Segev's biography... shows how central exclusionary <a href="/wiki/Nationalism" title="Nationalism">nationalism</a>, <a href="/wiki/War" title="War">war</a> and <a href="/wiki/Racism" title="Racism">racism</a> were to <a href="/wiki/David_Ben-Gurion" title="David Ben-Gurion">Ben-Gurion</a>'s vision of the <a href="/wiki/Jewish_homeland" class="mw-redirect" title="Jewish homeland">Jewish homeland</a> in <a href="/wiki/Palestine_(region)" title="Palestine (region)">Palestine</a>, and how contemptuous he was not only of the <a href="/wiki/Arabs" title="Arabs">Arabs</a> but of Jewish life outside <a href="/wiki/Zion" title="Zion">Zion</a>. [Liberal Jews] may look at the state that Ben-Gurion built, and ask if the cost has been worth it." (p.&#160;42 of Shatz's review.)</li>
